## Review

**6 out of 10, Needs substantial work.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

This is an investor reference document that reads as a disclosure pack more than a presentation. Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded. But most slides carry no message headline, only a topic label. Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column. Slide 14 is the one chart with a real claim title and an emphasized final bar, and the photo pages 16 to 25 are strong but make no argument. The truncated y-axis on slide 14 exaggerates the drop, and the core story is recoverable only with effort.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the consistent shaded latest-period column across the financial tables, the clear agenda on slide 2, the claim-led title and red highlight bar on slide 14, and the large, well-composed photo pages.

**Most useful repair:** Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

**What would change the score:** Trim the text and chart density on slides 9 to 11, add labels or takeaways to the unlabelled multi-chart slides 26 and 27, and give each photo page a one-line caption that states why it matters.

_Confidence medium. 36 of 36 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a summary of Q4 and full-year 2024 highlights covering profitability, cash and operations, followed by a single-page-per-topic financial summary of revenue, margins, EBITDA and cash flow. It then moves through operational data on production, deliveries, storage and Supercharger growth, vehicle capacity by region, and core technology updates on FSD, AI compute and battery work. Other highlights cover Energy and Services, followed by a forward-looking outlook on volume, cash, profit and products. Photo and product pages on Cybertruck, the new Model Y, Semi, Megafactory Shanghai and the lithium refinery come next, then detailed key metrics charts and full financial statements, reconciliations and a closing page of definitions and forward-looking statement disclaimers. Several slides are worth studying: the highlights summary uses short bold-labelled blocks of text, the financial summary tables use a consistent layout across quarterly and annual views, and the vehicle capacity slide pairs a plain table with a regional market share chart.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the Tesla deck say about installed annual vehicle capacity at the Shanghai factory?**

Slide 9 lists Shanghai capacity for Model 3 and Model Y as >950,000 vehicles per year, with status Production. The slide adds that installed capacity is not the same as the current production rate.

**What was Tesla's total revenue in Q4 2024 according to the deck?**

Slide 4 reports total revenues of 25,707 ($ in millions) for Q4-2024. Slide 6 states that total revenue increased 2% YoY in Q4 to $25.7B.

**How many slides does the Tesla Q4 and FY 2024 deck have and how is it structured?**

The deck has 36 slides. Slide 2 is the agenda, listing Highlights (slide 3), Financial Summary (slide 4), Operational Summary (slide 7), Vehicle Capacity (slide 9), Outlook (slide 12), Key Metrics (slide 26) and Financial Statements (slide 28).

**What does Tesla's outlook in the deck say about energy storage deployments in 2025?**

Slide 12 says Tesla expects energy storage deployments to grow at least 50% year-over-year in 2025.

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The highlights page groups key results under short labelled headings (Profitability, Cash, Operations), so a reader can scan the quarter's story in seconds.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

### Slide 4

**Review notes**

- Review: Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Review: Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

**Layout**

A single full-width financial table on a white slide. The section label FINANCIAL SUMMARY sits top-left at about 8.9% from the top, with the (Unaudited) subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned to a 4.6% left margin. The table runs from the left margin to about 95% of the width, with a 7-column grid (label column, then 2020 to 2024 and YoY) and 22 rows separated by blank spacer rows; the bottom row ends near 89% of the height. A faint page number 5 sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Design**

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Review: Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

**Text**

FINANCIAL SUMMARY
Revenue
Total revenue increased 2% YoY in Q4 to $25.7B. YoY, revenue was impacted by the following items:
+ growth in Energy Generation and Storage and Services and Other
+ growth in vehicle deliveries
+ higher regulatory credit revenue
- reduced S3XY vehicle average selling price (ASP) (excl. FX impact1), due to pricing, attractive financing options and mix
Profitability
Our operating income decreased 23% YoY to $1.6B in Q4, resulting in a 6.2% operating margin. YoY, operating income was primarily
impacted by the following items:
- reduced S3XY vehicle ASP
- increase in operating expenses driven by AI and other R&D projects
+ growth in Energy Generation and Storage and Services and Other gross profit
+ lower cost per vehicle, including lower raw material costs partially offset by lower fixed cost absorption from production decrease YoY
+ higher regulatory credit revenue
Cash
Quarter-end cash, cash equivalents and investments in Q4 was $36.6B. The sequential increase of $2.9B was primarily the result of positive
free cash flow of $2.0B.
6
(1) Impact is calculated on a constant currency basis. Actuals are compared against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from Q4’23.

**Layout**

Single text-only slide with a left label column and a right content column. The small spaced-caps title sits top-left at about 8.9% from the top, with a left margin of about 4.6%. Three rows (Revenue, Profitability, Cash) each have a bold label at x 4.5% and body text left-aligned at x 19.3%, with generous vertical gaps between rows. Footer at the bottom left holds the page number 6 and footnote (1) at about 95% of the height; a faint wordmark sits bottom right. No slide number beyond the footer number.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no gradient or imagery. Title is small, uppercase, widely letter-spaced Calibri regular in dark grey #333333, roughly 2% of slide height. Row labels are Calibri Bold in near-black #111111. Introductory sentences are Calibri regular in mid grey #6E6E6E. Bulleted items are Calibri regular in black #000000. Footnote and page number are light grey (#BABABA to #BFBFBF) at about 1.6% of slide height. No lines, shapes, corner radii, shadows or gradients are visible. Body text is about 2% of slide height, title about 2%, footer about 1.6%.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Review: Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

**Layout**

Single full-width data table on a white page. The title block OPERATIONAL SUMMARY with the subtitle (Unaudited) sits top-left at about x 4.6%, y 8.9%, left-aligned, with the table starting directly beneath it at y 14.0% and spanning x 4.6% to 95.4%. Columns are label (4.6% to 43.3%) followed by Q4-2023, Q1-2024, Q2-2024, Q3-2024, Q4-2024 and YoY, each about 8.7% wide, with numbers right-aligned. Rows are grouped by blank gaps into five blocks: production, deliveries, operating lease, storage and locations/fleet, and charging. The footer sits at the bottom with the page number at left (x 3.0%, y 95.0%) and the footnote beside it at x 5.2%, y 95.2%. A faint wordmark sits at bottom right. Margins are about 4.6% left and 4.6% right; the lower band of the slide below y 76% is empty.

Table. Columns: row label; Q4-2023; Q1-2024; Q2-2024; Q3-2024; Q4-2024 (highlighted in light pink); YoY. Header row: grey #7F7F7F fill with white centred column labels. Rows, in order: (1) Model 3/Y production: 476,777; 412,376; 386,576; 443,668; 436,718; -8%. (2) Other models production: 18,212; 20,995; 24,255; 26,128; 22,727; 25%. (3) Total production (bold): 494,989; 433,371; 410,831; 469,796; 459,445; -7%. Gap row. (4) Model 3/Y deliveries: 461,538; 369,783; 422,405; 439,975; 471,930; 2%. (5) Other models deliveries: 22,969; 17,027; 21,551; 22,915; 23,640; 3%. (6) Total deliveries (bold): 484,507; 386,810; 443,956; 462,890; 495,570; 2%. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, texture or imagery. Title OPERATIONAL SUMMARY in widely letter-spaced sans-serif capitals (Calibri, regular weight, about 2.0% of slide height, cap height roughly 1.4%), colour #333333. Subtitle (Unaudited) in Calibri regular, same size, #333333. Table body text Calibri regular in black #000000, about 2.0% of slide height; total rows Calibri bold. Header labels Calibri regular, white #FFFFFF on #7F7F7F. Row bands #F2F2F2 alternating with white. Q4-2024 column highlight light pink approximately #F2DCDB. Gap rows are white. Thin white separators between cells. No rounded corners, shadows or borders. Footer page number and footnote in light grey #BFBFBF Calibri at about 1.6% of slide height; superscript (1) at about 1.0%. Wordmark very pale grey approximately #D9D9D9.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Review: Its routing is clear: slide 2 lists the sections with page numbers, and the financial tables on slides 4 to 8 are consistently laid out with the latest column shaded.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The vehicle capacity table is paired with a regional market share chart, letting the capacity data and the market context read side by side.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.
- What would change the score: Trim the text and chart density on slides 9 to 11, add labels or takeaways to the unlabelled multi-chart slides 26 and 27, and give each photo page a one-line caption that states why it matters.

**Text**

VEHICLE CAPACITY
Preparations are underway across our existing factories for the launch of new products in 2025,
including more affordable models. New Model Y has launched on the configurator globally with
production ramp and deliveries beginning later in Q1.
US: California, Nevada and Texas
Semi Factory construction continued in Q4 and recently completed roof and wall enclosure of
the main building area. First truck builds are scheduled to start by end of 2025 with ramp
beginning in early 2026. Preparation is underway for Cybercab lines at Gigafactory Texas with
volume production planned for 2026.
Given its unique functionality – including Powershare, stainless steel exterior, enhanced
durability and bioweapon defense mode – we deployed a fleet of Cybertrucks equipped with
Starlink in the Los Angeles area to help first responders and those impacted by the fires get
access to electricity and Wi-Fi. We expect Cybertruck to be eligible for the IRA consumer tax
credit, helping to improve affordability and access for even more customers.
APAC: Shanghai
In Q4, we achieved record deliveries in China as Model Y became the best-selling vehicle for the
full year. Tesla also became the fastest growing brand in South Korea, and we launched vehicle
sales in the Philippines.
Europe: Berlin-Brandenburg
In 2024, Model Y was the best-selling vehicle of any type in Denmark, Norway, Sweden,
Switzerland and the Netherlands and we expect Model Y to have been the second best-selling
vehicle of any type in Europe. Tesla was the most sold brand in Norway for the fourth year in a
row with Model Y and Model 3 the best- and second-best selling cars of any type in 2024.
Current Installed Annual Vehicle Capacity
Region / Model / Capacity / Status
California / Model S / Model X / 100,000 / Production
 / Model 3 / Model Y / >550,000 / Production
Shanghai / Model 3 / Model Y / >950,000 / Production
Berlin / Model Y / >375,000 / Production
Texas / Model Y / >250,000 / Production
 / Cybertruck / >125,000 / Production
 / Cybercab / - / In development
Nevada / Tesla Semi / - / Pilot production
TBD / Roadster / - / In development
Installed capacity ≠ current production rate and there may be limitations discovered as production rates approach
capacity. Production rates depend on a variety of factors, including equipment uptime, component supply,
downtime related to factory upgrades, regulatory considerations and other factors.
US/Canada   Europe   China
4%

Market share of Tesla vehicles by region (TTM)
Source: Tesla estimates based on latest available data from ACEA; Autonews.com; CAAM – light-duty vehicles
only; TTM = Trailing twelve months

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: the left text column spans about 4.5% to 50% of the slide width from y 18.6% to 96%, with a large-caps section label at top-left, and the right column spans about 54.7% to 95.5% holding a table at top and a line chart with source line at bottom. Headings in the left column are left-aligned on the same left margin of 4.5%, separated by thin grey horizontal rules that run the width of the left column at about y 28.7%, 64.6% and 80.3%. The slide number 9 sits bottom-left at x 3% and y 95%; a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right near the source line; no title block sits at the top apart from the section label at y 8.9%.

**Visuals**

Table: titled 'Current Installed Annual Vehicle Capacity' (bold, centred above the table at about y 15.2%). Columns: Region, Model, Capacity, Status. Header row (grey #7F7F7F with white text) and 9 body rows (fill #F2F2F2). Rows in order: California | Model S / Model X | 100,000 | Production; (blank region) | Model 3 / Model Y | >550,000 | Production; Shanghai | Model 3 / Model Y | >950,000 | Production; Berlin | Model Y | >375,000 | Production; Texas | Model Y | >250,000 | Production; (blank) | Cybertruck | >125,000 | Production; (blank) | Cybercab | - | In development; Nevada | Tesla Semi | - | Pilot production; TBD | Roadster | - | In development. Model names bold, capacity right-aligned, status centred, region left-aligned. Chart: line chart titled 'Market share of Tesla vehicles by region (TTM)', placed below the table at about x 54.7% to 95.5%, y 62% to 90%. Legend at top-left of plot: US/Canada (blue line), Europe (red line), China (green line). Y axis: market share in percent, 0% to 4%, gridline and tick every 1% (labels 0%, 1%, 2%, 3%, 4%). X axis: 24 quarterly categories Q1 2019 to Q4 2024, labels rotated about 45 degrees. No values printed on the chart; estimated values (about): US/Canada rises from about 1.1% (Q1 2019) to a peak of about 3.95% (Q4 2023) and ends near 3.7% (Q4 2024); Europe starts about 0.3% (Q1 2019), reaches about 2.8% (Q3 2023) and ends about 2.5% (Q4 2024); China starts about 0.1% (Q1 2019), reaches about 2.4% (Q4 2023 to Q4 2024) and ends about 2.4%. Dips in US/Canada at Q4 2019 (about 1.05%). No highlighted points; lines are about 2 px wide with no markers. Source line below the chart in light grey. Photographs: none. Logos: a faint Tesla wordmark bottom-right.

**Design**

Background: Solid white (#FFFFFF) covering the whole slide. Plain white background (#FFFFFF) with no gradient, texture or imagery. Section label in widely letter-spaced uppercase sans-serif Calibri Regular, dark grey #333333. Headings in bold Calibri, near-black #111111. Body copy in regular Calibri, mid grey #6E6E6E, roughly 2.0% of slide height, with generous line spacing of about 1.9 times. Table header #7F7F7F with white text; body rows #F2F2F2 with black text; table text Calibri about 2.0% of slide height. Chart series colours: blue (US/Canada), red (Europe), green (China). Chart title and axis title in bold grey #7F7F7F; axis labels and legend in #595959. Notes and source lines in light grey #BFBFBF at about 1.6% of slide height. Thin divider rules in light grey (about #D9D9D9, 0.5 pt). No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.
- What would change the score: Trim the text and chart density on slides 9 to 11, add labels or takeaways to the unlabelled multi-chart slides 26 and 27, and give each photo page a one-line caption that states why it matters.

**Text**

CORE TECHNOLOGY
Artificial Intelligence Software and Hardware
In Q4, we completed the deployment of Cortex, a ~50k H100 training cluster at Gigafactory
Texas. Cortex helped enable V13 of FSD (Supervised)1, which boasts major improvements in
safety and comfort thanks to 4.2x increase in data, higher resolution video inputs, 2x reduction
in photon-to-control latency and redesigned controller, among other enhancements. FSD
(Supervised) can now start from park and perform unpark, reverse and park capabilities. In Q4,
Tesla vehicles using Autopilot technology drove 5.94 million miles between accidents2 – the best
Q4 ever – compared to the U.S. average of .70 million miles. Progress on Optimus hardware and
software continued in Q4, including the latest generation hand, robust locomotion and training
on additional tasks, ahead of planned pilot production in 2025.
Vehicle and Other Software
The holiday release included Apple Watch support - customers can pair their watch as a key to
unlock and lock their vehicle without ever pressing a button just like the phone key. With a
Premium Connectivity subscription, Dashcam and Sentry Mode clips can be viewed and
downloaded directly from the Tesla mobile app. An animated three-hour precipitation forecast
can be viewed on the map, and SiriusXM is now available to our full lineup with launch as a
native app for Model 3, Model Y and Cybertruck.
Battery, Powertrain and Manufacturing
We processed our first spodumene (lithium-containing concentrate) through the front-end of
the lithium refinery only 18 months after ground-breaking: much faster than any plant we know
of outside of Asia. The intermediate material was on-spec, and we are on track to commission
the plant in 2025. Our in-house 4680 cell hit a production rate exceeding 2.5k Cybertrucks/week.

**Layout**

Single-slide page on white with two columns: the left text column (about 4.6% to 50% of width) holds three bold section headings, each followed by a paragraph, separated by two thin grey horizontal rules at about 50% and 75% of height. The right column (about 55% to 95% of width) stacks an upper area chart (top about 19%, bottom about 43%) above a lower line chart (top about 56%, bottom about 83%), with the chart caption under each. The section title 'CORE TECHNOLOGY' is top-left at about 8.9% from top, left-aligned, with the page number at bottom-left (about 95% from top) and footnotes bottom-right above a faint wordmark.

**Visuals**

Two charts. (1) Stacked area chart titled 'Cumulative miles driven with FSD (Supervised) (billions)' (bold grey caption below the chart). X axis: quarterly date labels Jun-2021 to Dec-2024 at 3-month steps, rotated about 45 degrees. Y axis: 0.0 to 3.0 in steps of 0.5, unitless label, units in billions per caption. Series 1 'FSD Miles on V11 and Before' is a blue (#4F81BD) area from the baseline; values are about 0.0 at Jun-2021, about 0.1 at Mar-2023, about 0.5 at Sep-2023, about 0.95 from Mar-2024 to Dec-2024. Series 2 'FSD Miles on V12 and Beyond' is a red (#C00000) area stacked on top; values are about 0.3 at Mar-2024, about 1.3 at Jun-2024, about 2.1 at Sep-2024, about 2.9 at Dec-2024. No printed values. Legend at top inside plot: red square 'FSD Miles on V12 and Beyond' and blue square 'FSD Miles on V11 and Before', horizontal, grey text. No highlighted points. (2) Line chart with markers titled 'Million miles driven before an accident occurs' (bold grey caption below). X axis: Q4 2019, Q4 2020, Q4 2021, Q4 2022, Q4 2023, Q4 2024. Y axis: 0 to 6 in steps of 1, unit million miles. Series 'Tesla vehicles using Autopilot technology' red (#C00000) line with diamond markers: about 3.1, 3.75, 4.35, 4.85, 5.4, 5.94 (5.94 printed in text). Series 'Tesla vehicles not using Autopilot technology' blue (#4F81BD) with diamond markers: about 1.5, 1.25, 1.5, 1.4, 1.0, 1.1. Series 'U.S. avg' dark grey (#595959) with diamond markers: about 0.5, 0.55, 0.65, 0.67, 0.67, 0.7 (.70 printed in text for Q4 2024 comparison). Legend below plot in one row: red diamond line 'Tesla vehicles using Autopilot technology', blue diamond line 'Tesla vehicles not using Autopilot technology', grey diamond line 'U.S. avg'. Light grey horizontal gridlines on both charts. Faint wordmark bottom-right is a Tesla logo, very small, light grey.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background #FFFFFF with no decoration. Section title in Calibri regular, widely letter-spaced capitals, colour #333333. Section headings in Calibri Bold, near-black #111111. Body paragraphs in Calibri regular, mid grey #6E6E6E. Chart captions in Calibri Bold, grey #7E7E7E. Chart tick labels and legends in Calibri regular, #595959. Footnote text in Calibri, light grey #BFBFBF; hyperlink in blue #0000FF underlined. Page number in Calibri, #BABABA. Title size about 2.0% of slide height, body about 2.0% of slide height, chart labels about 1.6%. Thin light grey horizontal rules (about #C8C8C8, 1 px). Area and line series use flat fills and solid lines with no shadows or gradients; line markers are small filled diamonds.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.
- What would change the score: Trim the text and chart density on slides 9 to 11, add labels or takeaways to the unlabelled multi-chart slides 26 and 27, and give each photo page a one-line caption that states why it matters.

**Text**

OTHER HIGHLIGHTS
Our Energy business continued to grow rapidly in 2024 as we expand capacity for both
Megapack and Powerwall to meet demand. Services and Other – a collection of businesses that
support new vehicle sales – achieved its third year in a row of profitability in 2024.
Energy Generation and Storage
The Energy business achieved record deployments for both Powerwall and Megapack at a
combined 11.0 GWh, resulting in a record gross profit in Q4. Material and other costs continued
to come down in Q4 at the Lathrop Megafactory. Both Powerwall and Megapack continue to be
supply constrained as we open new markets and demand for energy storage products continues
to grow. With construction completed, Shanghai Megafactory will begin ramping in Q1.
Services and Other
In Q4, we added over 3k Supercharger stalls to the network and delivered 1.4 TWh of energy.
We unveiled our V4 cabinet, which supports 400V-1,000V vehicle architectures, charges up to
500kW for passenger vehicles and 1.2MW for Tesla Semi and has cutting-edge power electronics
with 3x the power density. We launched Battery Heating at Superchargers, a feature that gets
vehicles with LFP battery packs back on the road up to 4x faster. We also continued to welcome
more OEMs to the North American Supercharger network, including the first NACS native
vehicles. Overall, in 2024, we launched Superchargers in three new countries, added over 10k
new Supercharger stalls, and grew the network by 19% year-over-year, for a total of 65k+
Superchargers worldwide. We delivered 5.2+ TWh, offsetting over 5.5 billion kg of direct CO2
emissions and 2.4 billion liters of gasoline.
Energy and Services and Other gross profit ($M)
Energy Storage deployments (GWh)
11

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: left text column spans about x 4.5% to 50% with headings and body paragraphs; right column spans about x 53% to 95% and holds two stacked bar charts, the upper one at about y 18% to 52% and the lower at about y 57% to 91%. The title 'OTHER HIGHLIGHTS' sits top-left at about y 9%, left-aligned with the left column at x 4.6%. Two thin horizontal rules run across the left column at about y 28.6% and y 52.2%. Page number '11' sits bottom-left at about x 3%, y 95%; a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right at about x 91%, y 94%. Margins are about 4.5% left and about 5% right.

**Visuals**

Two vertical bar charts, both single-series, red bars, no legend, no highlighted points. Chart 1: title 'Energy and Services and Other gross profit ($M)' printed beneath the chart; y-axis labelled in $M with gridlines at 1,000, 800, 600, 400, 200, 0, -200, -400, -600, -800, -1,000 (range -1,000 to 1,000); x-axis categories Q1 2018 through Q4 2024 (28 quarters) rotated about 45 degrees; bars are about: Q1 2018 -70, Q2 2018 -70, Q3 2018 -50, Q4 2018 -90, Q1 2019 -180, Q2 2019 -90, Q3 2019 -30, Q4 2019 -40, Q1 2020 -70, Q2 2020 -40, Q3 2020 -30, Q4 2020 -180, Q1 2021 -170, Q2 2021 -10, Q3 2021 -10, Q4 2021 -40, Q1 2022 -70, Q2 2022 150, Q3 2022 170, Q4 2022 250, Q1 2023 300, Q2 2023 440, Q3 2023 510, Q4 2023 370, Q1 2024 480, Q2 2024 900, Q3 2024 970, Q4 2024 890 (all values estimated, marked about, as the slide prints no bar values). Chart 2: title 'Energy Storage deployments (GWh)' printed beneath; y-axis 0 to 12 GWh, unit labels 0 to 12 at intervals of 1; x-axis categories Q1 2018 through Q4 2024 rotated; bars are about: Q1 2018 0.4, Q2 2018 0.3, Q3 2018 0.3, Q4 2018 0.3, Q1 2019 0.3, Q2 2019 0.5, Q3 2019 0.5, Q4 2019 0.6, Q1 2020 0.3, Q2 2020 0.4, Q3 2020 0.8, Q4 2020 1.6, Q1 2021 0.5, Q2 2021 1.3, Q3 2021 1.3, Q4 2021 1.0, Q1 2022 0.9, Q2 2022 1.1, Q3 2022 2.1, Q4 2022 2.4, Q1 2023 3.9, Q2 2023 3.7, Q3 2023 4.0, Q4 2023 3.2, Q1 2024 4.1, Q2 2024 9.5, Q3 2024 7.0, Q4 2024 about 11.0 (all estimated). No legend. Bars are thin, about one third of category width, with the chart area showing light horizontal gridlines. Photographs and illustrations: none.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate slide with no decoration. Titles and headings are dark, body copy is mid grey, chart titles are grey and bold. Title 'OTHER HIGHLIGHTS' in widely letter-spaced uppercase Calibri regular, #333333. Section headings in Calibri Bold, #111111, about 2.0% of slide height. Body text Calibri regular, #6E6E6E, about 2.0% of slide height (about 1.5 times the size of the chart labels). Chart axis labels Calibri regular #595959 at about 1.6% of slide height. Bar fill #C0504D, flat, no outline, no shadow. Divider rules are thin light grey lines about 1 px, approximately #BFBFBF. No corner radii, gradients or shadows.

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The outlook is split into labelled sections for volume, cash, profit and product, giving each forward-looking claim its own clear heading.
- Review: Slides 3, 6, 9, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraph pages in small grey type, and slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 are small-type tables with no takeaway marked beyond the shaded column.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide titles as takeaways, break the paragraph-heavy slides 3, 9, 10, 11 and 12 into fewer, larger points with the key numbers pulled out, and raise the table and chart text size on slides 4 to 8 and 26 to 27.

**Text**

OUTLOOK
Volume
With the advancements in vehicle autonomy and the introduction of new products, we expect the vehicle business to return to growth in / 2025. The rate of growth will depend on a variety of factors, including the rate of acceleration of our autonomy efforts, production ramp at / our factories and the broader macroeconomic environment. We expect energy storage deployments to grow at least 50% year-over-year in / 2025.
Cash
We have sufficient liquidity to fund our product roadmap, long-term capacity expansion plans and other expenses. Furthermore, we will / manage the business such that we maintain a strong balance sheet during this uncertain period.
Profit
While we continue to execute on innovations to reduce the cost of manufacturing and operations, over time, we expect our hardware- / related profits to be accompanied by an acceleration of AI, software and fleet-based profits.
Product
Plans for new vehicles, including more affordable models, remain on track for start of production in the first half of 2025. These vehicles / will utilize aspects of the next generation platform as well as aspects of our current platforms and will be produced on the same / manufacturing lines as our current vehicle line-up. / This approach will result in achieving less cost reduction than previously expected but enables us to prudently grow our vehicle volumes in / a more capex efficient manner during uncertain times. This should help us fully utilize our current expected maximum capacity of close to / three million vehicles, enabling more than 60% growth over 2024 production before investing in new manufacturing lines. / Our purpose-built Robotaxi product – Cybercab – will continue to pursue a revolutionary “unboxed” manufacturing strategy and is / scheduled for volume production starting in 2026.
12

### Slide 14

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A single line chart of COGS per vehicle with the all-time low called out in the title makes the one message of the slide immediate.
- Review: Slide 14 is the one chart with a real claim title and an emphasized final bar, and the photo pages 16 to 25 are strong but make no argument.
- Review: The truncated y-axis on slide 14 exaggerates the drop, and the core story is recoverable only with effort.
- Worth keeping: Keep the consistent shaded latest-period column across the financial tables, the clear agenda on slide 2, the claim-led title and red highlight bar on slide 14, and the large, well-composed photo pages.

**Text**

AVERAGE COGS PER VEHICLE* ACHIEVED AN ALL–TIME LOW IN Q4 2024 / $39,000 / $38,000 / $37,000 / $36,000 / $35,000 / $34,000 / $33,000 / $32,000 / Q1 2023 / Q2 2023 / Q3 2023 / Q4 2023 / Q1 2024 / Q2 2024 / Q3 2024 / Q4 2024 / 14 / * Calculated by dividing Cost of Automotive Sales Revenue by respective quarter’s new deliveries (ex-operating leases)

### Slide 15

**Text**

CYBERTRUCK -
ADVANCEMENTS IN VEHICLE TECHNOLOGY AND ARCHITECTURE
Technology
Description
Use in Future Vehicles
Manufacturing and Design
Stainless Steel Exoskeleton
• Cold-rolled full hard stainless steel exoskeleton carries crash loads and increases torsional stiffness of the body
• Iconic visual design; high dent- and corrosion- resistance reduce concerns over daily wear and tear
Air Bending Manufacturing Process
• First-of-its-kind automated tooling developed by Tesla that can bend full hard stainless steel without touching it
• Enables the use of full hard stainless steel as an A-surface without getting scratched by tooling
Giga-castings
• Our largest castings ever - custom alloy and a 9,000-ton press combine to make front and rear vehicle structures
• Reduces number of parts and manufacturing complexity, and, as a bonus, scrap is fully recyclable
✓
Integrated Audio with Body Structure
• Utilizes the body structure as the reactive volume for dual subwoofers
• Giant sound in extremely dense packaging
✓
Powertrain and Electrical Architecture
48-Volt Electrical Architecture
• 48-volt electrical system reduces current by 4x and heat generated by 16x compared to typical automotive voltage
• Reduction in vehicle harness reduces vehicle weight, increases energy efficiency and simplifies electrical systems
✓
800-Volt Battery System
• Higher voltage powertrain is more energy efficient for high power vehicles such as Cybertruck and Tesla Semi
• Enables smaller cables which reduces cost and mass while also enabling higher power (500kW) charging
✓
Etherloop Communication Architecture
• Gigabit ethernet communication is 1,000x faster than traditional automotive communication standards
• Enables distributed controller architecture which improves redundancy and eliminates 70% of cross-vehicle wires
✓
Bidirectional Charging (Powershare)
• The Cybertruck can provide power to your jobsite, home or other devices (Vehicle-to-Grid or Vehicle-to-Home)
• Turns vehicles into mobile energy storage units with up to 11.5kW of onboard power, enabling hours of work
✓
Ride and Comfort
Custom Laminated Glass
• Novel glass and laminate developed by Tesla for increased comfort and strength
• Improves sound isolation and blocks harmful UV, keeping the cabin cool while maintaining a panoramic view
✓
Adaptive Air Suspension
• Adjusts ride height and damping for different terrains with 12” of adjustment getting up to 16” of ground clearance
• Improves on-road comfort and stability without sacrificing off-road capability
✓
Steer-by-Wire
• Replaces traditional steer shaft with redundant controllers and motors, enabling an adjustable steering ratio for different speeds and driving styles
• Reduces the amount you need to turn the wheel, enabling more agility at low speeds and more stability at high speeds
✓
Rear Wheel Steering
• Leveraging steer-by-wire, enables approximately ten degrees of steering on the rear wheels
• Improves turning radius at low speeds and increases stability at high speeds
15
TESLA

**Layout**

Single full-width table slide on a white field, 16:9. Title sits top-left at about 8.9% of slide height, starting at x 4.6%, in one tracked caps line with a dark prefix and grey remainder. The table runs from x 4.6% to 95.5% and y 12.2% to 93.3% with four columns: section label (4.6 to 14.9), Technology (14.9 to 28.5), Description (28.5 to 82.4), Use in Future Vehicles (82.4 to 95.5). Column headers sit on one row at y 13.7%, left-aligned, above a horizontal rule at y 16.3%. Left and right margins are about 4.6%; the page number sits bottom-left at x 3.0%, y 95.0%, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

A text-and-check table, no photographs or charts. Columns: Section (Manufacturing and Design; Powertrain and Electrical Architecture; Ride and Comfort), Technology (name of each technology), Description (two bullet points per technology, 3 to 4 bullets in one row only where wrapped), Use in Future Vehicles (grey check mark ✓ or blank). Rows in order: Stainless Steel Exoskeleton (blank); Air Bending Manufacturing Process (blank); Giga-castings (check); Integrated Audio with Body Structure (check); 48-Volt Electrical Architecture (check); 800-Volt Battery System (check); Etherloop Communication Architecture (check); Bidirectional Charging (Powershare) (check); Custom Laminated Glass (check); Adaptive Air Suspension (check); Steer-by-Wire (check); Rear Wheel Steering (check). Check marks are all present except the first two rows. Thin dark grey horizontal rules separate rows, a heavier rule separates sections, and thin vertical rules separate the four columns. No highlights.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title: first part 'CYBERTRUCK -' in dark grey #333333 Calibri Bold, tracked wide caps; second part in mid grey #888888 Calibri Bold, tracked wide caps. Column headers and section labels in black #000000 Calibri Bold. Technology names in black #000000 Calibri regular. Description bullets in grey #6E6E6E Calibri regular with ArialMT grey bullets #6E6E6E. Check marks grey #6E6E6E Segoe UI Symbol. Title height about 2.0% of slide height, body text about 1.8%, headers about 2.0%, page number about 1.6%. Rules thin dark grey (#333333 approx., 1 px). No corner radii, shadows or gradients. Sans-serif throughout.

### Slide 16

**Layout**

Two equal square photographs sit side by side in a two-column arrangement beneath a single-line letter-spaced title at top left; the title starts at x 4.6% and the images span from y 12.1% to 92.9% with a gutter of about 5.1% between them and margins of about 4.6% left and right. **Visuals**

Two photographs of the new Tesla Model Y side by side, each boxed as a sharp-cornered square image. Left image: the car seen from the rear three-quarter view on the right, showing the full-width illuminated red light bar across the tail, a silver-grey body, black roof panel, five-spoke silver alloy wheels and a dark rear bumper; the car is lit from the rear with a red glow on the floor. Right image: the car seen from the front three-quarter view on the left, showing a glowing white light bar across the front, a silver-grey body, dark glass roof and windows, a silver side mirror, five-spoke silver alloy wheels and a Tesla badge on the front fender; lit from the front with a cool white highlight. Both photographs are studio shots on a dark navy-to-black gradient background with a soft blue light beam rising from the upper right; composition is centred on the car with the car occupying the lower-middle of each frame, not cropped, and the floor fades to near-black. Dominant colours: near-black #0A0F1A, navy #1B2A3F, silver-grey #A9AFB7, red accent #C8102E on the left image.

Body text is absent apart from the page number in light grey #BABABA at about 1.6% of slide height. No rules, borders or shadows; the photographs are flat with square corners and no overlay.

Background: exposed silver ducting, steel platforms with red railings, cylindrical silver ventilation hoods and a green exit sign. ### Slide 25

**Text**

CORTEX ~50K GPU TRAINING CLUSTER
25

**Layout**

Single-column composition on a white page. A small letter-spaced title sits top-left at the left margin, and a wide landscape photograph spans from the left margin to the right margin below it. The page number sits bottom-left and a faint wordmark logo sits bottom-right, both outside the photo. Left and right margins are about 4.6% of the slide width.

**Visuals**

One photograph, not cropped and not full-bleed, placed as a rectangle inside the margins. Subject: a long aisle inside a data centre filled with tall black server racks. The racks on the right in the foreground are densely populated with server nodes, with red and blue cables and silver liquid-cooling manifolds with brass fittings at the bottom. The rows recede to the left and right, with glass-fronted cabinets and an empty grey-tiled aisle on the left. Overhead, a grey wire-mesh cable tray runs the length of the room with black cables hanging down onto the racks. Composition: eye-level, wide landscape frame, racks in the right two-thirds, the aisle vanishing point near the centre-left. Lighting: even, neutral overhead lighting, no visible shadows dominating. Dominant colours: black and charcoal racks, light grey ceiling and floor tiles, red and teal cable accents. No overlay or tint. Also a faint grey wordmark logo at the bottom-right, low contrast, not legible as text.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background #FFFFFF with no gradient or pattern. Title in bold Calibri-style humanist sans-serif, all capitals, wide letter-spacing, about 2.0% of slide height for capitals. 'CORTEX' is dark grey #333333 and '~50K GPU TRAINING CLUSTER' is mid grey #888888. Page number in regular Calibri, light grey #BABABA, about 1.6% of slide height. The photograph has no border, shadow or corner radius. No body text, no rules or shapes.

**Layout**

Three equal-width bar charts sit side by side in a three-column row spanning roughly the middle of the slide, with a title block top-left above them. **Visuals**

Three vertical bar charts, all showing quarterly data from 1Q-2022 to 4Q-2024 (12 quarters each, x-axis labels rotated 90 degrees). Chart 1: Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units); y-axis 0.0 to 0.5 in steps of 0.1 with light grey gridlines; single series of blue bars with a vertical gradient from about #2F5C8F at the top to about #3D72AD at the middle; no legend; no printed values; about values: 1Q-2022 0.31, 2Q-2022 0.25, 3Q-2022 0.34, 4Q-2022 0.40, 1Q-2023 0.42, 2Q-2023 0.47, 3Q-2023 0.43, 4Q-2023 0.48, 1Q-2024 0.38, 2Q-2024 0.44, 3Q-2024 0.46, 4Q-2024 0.49. **Layout**

Three bar charts sit side by side in three equal columns across the slide, each with its own y-axis at the left of the panel and rotated category labels beneath. The title block sits top-left at about 9 percent from the top with the margin at about 4.6 percent from the left; the captions and legends sit below each chart, and a faint page number sits bottom-left with a faint wordmark bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Three clustered column charts, each with horizontal gridlines and no chart border. Panel 1: Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units), single series, y-axis 0.0 to 2.0 in steps of 0.2, x-axis 1Q-2022 to 4Q-2024 (12 quarters), values about 1.06, 1.11, 1.21, 1.31, 1.42, 1.63, 1.72, 1.80, 1.77, 1.74, 1.77, 1.78; no legend; bars steel blue. Panel 2: Operating Cash Flow ($B) and Free Cash Flow ($B), y-axis 0 to 20 in steps of 2, values for OCF about 13.8, 14.0, 16.0, 14.7, 13.2, 13.9, 12.1, 13.2, 11.0, 11.5, 14.4, 14.9 and FCF about 6.9, 6.9, 8.9, 7.5, 5.7, 6.1, 3.6, 4.3, 1.3, 1.7, 3.5, 3.5; legend below with blue square Operating Cash Flow ($B) and red square Free Cash Flow ($B); no value labels printed, values are estimates. Panel 3: Net Income ($B) and Adjusted EBITDA ($B), y-axis 0 to 20 in steps of 2, Net Income about 8.4, 9.5, 11.1, 12.5, 11.7, 12.1, 10.7, 14.9, 13.6, 12.3, 12.7, 7.1 and Adjusted EBITDA about 14.8, 16.1, 17.9, 19.2, 18.4, 19.3, 18.1, 16.6, 15.7, 14.8, 15.6, 16.6; legend below with blue square Net Income ($B) and red square Adjusted EBITDA ($B); values are estimates. No highlighted points. Bars are thin paired columns with a narrow gap.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background, flat with no shadows or gradients. Title in spaced capitals: KEY METRICS in dark grey #333333 bold Calibri, TRAILING 12 MONTHS (TTM) in mid grey #888888 bold Calibri, letter-spaced widely. **Layout**

Single full-width financial table on a plain white slide with the title block at top left, starting at x 4.6% and y 8.9%, and the table spanning x 4.5% to 95.4% from y 14.9% to 91.5%. Row labels are left-aligned at x 4.5% and the five numeric columns are right-aligned under the quarter headers Q4-2023 to Q4-2024; the Q4-2024 column is set off by a pink highlight band. Page number 29 sits bottom left at x 3.0%, y 95.0%, and a faint wordmark sits bottom right.

**Visuals**

Table with 5 numeric columns (Q4-2023, Q1-2024, Q2-2024, Q3-2024, Q4-2024) and one label column, about 37 rows including section headers (REVENUES, COST OF REVENUES, OPERATING EXPENSES) and an empty spacer row. Row values are exactly as listed in the text element. Subtotal rows (Total automotive revenues, Total revenues, Total automotive cost of revenues, Total cost of revenues, Gross profit, Total operating expenses, INCOME FROM OPERATIONS, INCOME BEFORE INCOME TAXES, NET INCOME, NET INCOME ATTRIBUTABLE TO COMMON STOCKHOLDERS) are bold or indented as printed. Highlighted points: the Q4-2024 column is shaded pale pink across its rows, and the Q4-2024 cells for Interest income through NET INCOME ATTRIBUTABLE show the highlight. No chart, photograph or diagram.

Title text is dark grey #333333 in widely letter-spaced Calibri regular capitals, subtitle in the same colour and style. Table body text is black #000000 in Calibri regular, with bold Calibri for totals. Section header rows and thin row bands use very light grey #F2F2F2. The Q4-2024 column highlight is pale pink #F2DCDB. Page number is light grey #BABABA. Title size is about 2.0% of slide height, table body about 1.7%, page number about 1.6%. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, texture or imagery. Title 'STATEMENT OF CASH FLOWS' in widely letter-spaced regular Calibri sans-serif, dark grey #333333, about 2.0% of slide height, with the subtitle '(Unaudited)' in the same colour and family below. Table body in regular Calibri sans-serif, black #000000, about 1.7% of slide height, with totals rows in Calibri Bold. Section header rows use a light grey fill #F2F2F2 with black uppercase text. Pale pink accent #F2DCDB fills the Q4-2024 column. Page number in light grey #BABABA. Shapes are square-cornered with no shadows or borders beyond thin white row gaps.

A single-column financial reconciliation table on a plain white slide. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white slide with no decoration. The title is set in a letter-spaced regular sans-serif (Calibri) in capitals, dark grey #333333, at about 2.0% of slide height. The subtitle '(Unaudited)' is Calibri regular in #333333, same size. Table body text is Calibri regular in black #000000 at about 1.7% of slide height, with bold Calibri for subtotal rows. Spacer bands are #F2F2F2. The 2024 column highlight is a pale pink, about #F2DCDC. Rules are hairline light grey. Page number in Calibri #BABABA. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

**Layout**

Single white page with a letter-spaced all-caps title and a '(Unaudited)' subtitle left-aligned at the top left margin (about 4.6% from the left edge). Three stacked financial tables sit below: row labels are left-aligned in a wide left column and numeric columns are right-aligned across the width, with the last column ending near 95.6% of the slide width. A small footer at bottom left carries the page number and the TTM footnote, and a faint wordmark sits at bottom right.

**Visuals**

A set of four stacked financial tables, with no charts or images. Table 1 (quarterly, 2Q-2021 to 4Q-2024, 15 columns): header 'In millions of USD'; rows Net cash provided by operating activities (GAAP), Capital expenditures, Free cash flow (non-GAAP). Table 2 (quarterly, 2Q-2021 to 4Q-2024, 15 columns): header 'In millions of USD'; rows Net income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP), Interest expense, Provision for (benefit from) income taxes, Depreciation, amortization and impairment, Stock-based compensation expense, Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP). Table 3 (trailing twelve months, 1Q-2022 to 4Q-2024, 12 columns): rows Net cash provided by operating activities – TTM (GAAP), Capital expenditures – TTM, Free cash flow – TTM (non-GAAP). Table 4 (trailing twelve months, 1Q-2022 to 4Q-2024, 12 columns): rows Net income attributable to common stockholders – TTM (GAAP), Interest expense – TTM, Provision for (benefit from) income taxes – TTM, Depreciation, amortization and impairment – TTM, Stock-based compensation expense – TTM, Adjusted EBITDA – TTM (non-GAAP). Negative values are in parentheses. Two light grey full-width bands separate the blocks inside table 1 and table 3 as spacer rows. Thin hairline rules sit under each row. No highlighted cells.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients, shadows or corner radii. Title and subtitle in dark grey #333333 Calibri regular, with the title in all capitals and very wide letter-spacing. Table body text is black #000000 Calibri regular. Accents are light grey #F2F2F2 spacer bands and hairline rules in about #BFBFBF. Footer text is mid-grey #808080 Calibri regular; the faint wordmark is light grey #D9D9D9. The title is about 2.0% of slide height and the table body about 1.7% of slide height. Sans-serif throughout.

### Slide 35

**Text**

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
WEBCAST INFORMATION
Tesla will provide a live webcast of its fourth quarter 2024 financial results conference call beginning at 4:30 p.m. CT on January 29, 2025 at ir.tesla.com. This webcast will also be available for replay for approximately one year thereafter.
CERTAIN TERMS
When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product deployment volume includes both customer units when installed and equipment sales at time of delivery. "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based compensation expense. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding operating leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant period's deliveries and using trading days. Constant currency impacts are calculated by comparing actuals against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from the prior period.
NON-GAAP FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
Certain statements in this update, including, but not limited to, statements in the “Outlook” section; statements relating to the development, strategy, ramp, production and capacity, demand and market growth, cost, pricing and profitability, investment, deliveries, deployment, availability and other features and improvements and timing of existing and future Tesla products and services; statements regarding operating margin, operating profits, spending and liquidity; and statements regarding expansion, improvements and/or ramp and related timing at our factories and refinery are “forward-looking statements” within the meaning of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. Forward-looking statements are based on assumptions and management’s current expectations, involve certain risks and uncertainties, and are not guarantees. Future results may differ materially from those expressed in any forward-looking statement. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: the risk of delays in launching and/or manufacturing our products, services and features cost-effectively; our ability to build and/or grow our products and services, sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; our ability to successfully and timely develop, introduce and scale, as well as our consumers’ demand for, products and services based on artificial intelligence, robotics and automation, electric vehicles, Autopilot and FSD (Supervised) features, and ride-hailing services generally and our vehicles and services specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at our factories; our ability to ramp our factories in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international expansion; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition in the automotive, transportation and energy product and services markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel; our ability to maintain the security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our annual report on Form 10-K filed with the SEC on January 29, 2024 and subsequent quarterly reports on Form 10-Q. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events or otherwise.

- body at x 4.6, y 40.1, w 90.5, h 14.5 [Calibri, regular, normal case; font size 1.58% of slide height; #000000; left aligned]: Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to / common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, / Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management / believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that / presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial / metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with / financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above. (Seven lines, line pitch about 2.2% of slide height, full column width.)

- text at x 4.6, y 19.6, w 5.9, h 1.6 [Calibri-Bold; font size 1.58; #000000]: CERTAIN TERMS
- text at x 4.6, y 22.8, w 90.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product deployment volume includes
- text at x 4.6, y 25.0, w 90.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: both customer units when installed and equipment sales at time of delivery. "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation,
- text at x 4.6, y 27.2, w 90.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based compensation expense. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding operating leases).
- text at x 4.6, y 29.3, w 90.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the
- text at x 4.6, y 31.5, w 90.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant period's deliveries and using trading days. Constant currency impacts
- text at x 4.6, y 33.6, w 46.9, h 1.6 [Calibri; font size 1.58; #000000]: are calculated by comparing actuals against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from the prior period.

### Slide 36

**Text**

T E S L A

**Layout**

A single centred logo wordmark on an otherwise empty dark slide; no title, body text, footer or slide number. The wordmark sits at the horizontal and vertical centre of the slide with generous empty margins on all sides.

**Visuals**

One logo: the Tesla wordmark, rendered as five separate letterforms (T, E, S, L, A) set in widely spaced, letter-spaced geometric capitals in flat grey. The letters are small relative to the slide and read left to right. No photograph, chart or other graphic.

**Design**

Background: Solid near-black #111111 fill covering the entire slide. Near-black background (#111111) with no gradient, texture or pattern. The wordmark is flat mid-grey (about #8A8A8A) with no shadow or outline. No titles or body text; the only typography is the spaced wordmark letters in a squared geometric sans-serif style, regular weight. Letters are roughly 3 percent of slide height. No lines, shapes or corner radii.
